Figure 1:
Typing rules for the Functional Machine Calculus

4
Simple types

Simple types for the FMC
[
6
]
describe the input/output behaviour of the stack machine. The type system has three levels, mirroring the syntactic categories of the machine: types
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
for terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, type vectors
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
(or
stack types
) for stacks
S
, and location-indexed families of type vectors
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
(or
memory types
) for memories
S_{A}
. A function type is then an implication between an input memory type and an output memory type.

Definition 12
.

FMC-types

{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\upsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
over a set of
base types

\Sigma
are given by:

{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\Coloneqq~{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\alpha\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\in\Sigma~\mid~{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\qquad{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\Coloneqq~\{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mid a\in A\}\qquad{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\Coloneqq~{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Equivalently, one may view a function type as an implication between two vectors of location-indexed types, considered modulo the permutation of types on different locations.

{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\sigma_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})\dots a_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\sigma_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})~{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}~b_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})\dots b_{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\tau_{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\qquad\qquad{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\sigma)\,b(\tau)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\sim{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}b(\tau)\,a(\sigma)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

We introduce the following notation, which will enable us to write types also in the manner above. The
empty
type vector is
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, and the empty memory type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\varepsilon_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. A
singleton
memory type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is empty at every location except
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, where it has
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
:
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})_{b}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\varepsilon\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
for
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\neq{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}b\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. A singleton
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\lambda(\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
on the main location
\lambda
may be written as
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
Concatenation
of type vectors is denoted by juxtaposition and the
reverse
of a type vector
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is written
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. This extends point-wise to families, so
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mid a\in A\}
and
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}=\{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mid a\in A\}
.

Definition 13
.

A
judgement

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is a typed term in a
context

\Gamma={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, a finite function from variables to types. The typing rules for the FMC are given in Figure
1
.

Example 14
.

The terms from Example
2
can be typed as follows, where
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\mathbb{Z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is a base type of integers. Recall that the first term adds a random number to the cell
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, and the second sends the numbers from zero to two to output, leaving the number three on the main stack.

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\langle x\rangle.\,[x].\,c\langle y\rangle.\,[y].\,+.\,\langle z\rangle.\,[z]c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\mathsf{rnd}(\mathbb{Z})\,c(\mathbb{Z}){\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}c(\mathbb{Z})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[\langle x\rangle.\,[x]\mathsf{out}.\,[x].\,[1].\,+].\,\langle f\rangle.\,[0].\,f.\,f.\,f\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\mathsf{out}(\mathbb{Z}\,\mathbb{Z}\,\mathbb{Z})\,\mathbb{Z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Note, there are two typing rules for variables: one for variables of base type, and one for variables of higher type. The simply-typed FMC satisfies the subject reduction property
[
6
]
, which is implicitly used in the following section.

5
Strong normalization

We will show that reduction for the simply-typed FMC is strongly normalizing (SN). Our proof is a variant of Gandy’s for the simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus
[
3
]
. Gandy’s proof interprets terms in domains of strictly ordered, strict monotone functionals: the base domain is
\mathbb{N}^{<}=(\mathbb{N},<_{\mathbb{N}})
, and if
X=(|X|,<_{X})
and
Y=(|Y|,<_{Y})
are domains then so is
X\to Y
where

\begin{array}[]{rcl}|X\to Y|&{=}&\{f\in Y^{X}\mid\forall x,x^{\prime}\in X.~x<_{X}x^{\prime}\implies f(x)<_{Y}f(x^{\prime})\}\\
f<_{X\to Y}g&{\iff}&\forall x\in X.~f(x)<g(x)~.\end{array}

The interpretation takes types to domains and terms of a given type to elements of that domain. The domains are well-founded and the interpretation of terms is such that it decreases on reduction, giving SN. One may further collapse a functional to a natural number to give an overestimate of the longest reduction sequence of a term. The literature has several variants on this proof, including one by De Vrijer that calculates longest reduction sequences exactly
[
2
]
; see also
[
18
,
15
,
14
]
.

We introduce a (to the best of our knowledge) new variant, that avoids the domain of
strict
functionals and instead interprets terms in the—more standard—domain of (non-strict) monotone functionals, as above but with
\leq
, generated from
\mathbb{N}^{\leq}=(\mathbb{N},\leq_{\mathbb{N}})
with
\to
. This domain is not well-founded, but our interpretation of terms ensures that when functionals are collapsed to a natural number, this strictly decreases upon reduction, giving SN.

The technical difference is small and subtle. Gandy’s proof originates in
\Lambda I
, where abstracted variables must occur, and hence the interpretation of an abstraction
\lambda x.M
is naturally strict: the argument to
x
always contributes to the overall interpretation. To generalize to the
\lambda
-calculus, where
x
need not occur in
\lambda x.M
, a construction is introduced to nevertheless measure the argument to
x
, so that the functional for
\lambda x.M
remains strictly monotone. The literature has several further such constructions
[
4
]
.

This solves the challenge of accounting for reduction in terms that will be discarded, common in SN proofs. In our proof, instead we account for such terms when they are supplied as arguments: for a term
M\,N
we increment the overall measure with that for
N
, measuring potential reduction in
N
even if it will be discarded by
M
. An abstraction
\lambda x.M
may then be interpreted as a standard monotone functional, avoiding strictness.

To build our domains, we use the
\to
construction above, as well as the product of domains
X\times Y
and an indexed product
\Pi_{a\in A}\,X_{a}
, defined in the expected way, as follows. Note, we will omit to work with base types in this section, so the base case is given by
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}({\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

\displaystyle|X\times Y|

\displaystyle=|X|\times|Y|\qquad

\displaystyle(x,y)\leq_{X\times Y}(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})

\displaystyle\iff x\leq_{X}x^{\prime}\wedge y\leq_{Y}y^{\prime}

\displaystyle|\Pi_{a\in A}\,X_{a}|

\displaystyle=\Pi_{a\in A}\,|X_{a}|

\displaystyle x\leq_{\Pi_{a\in A}X_{a}}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\iff\forall a\in A.~x_{a}\leq_{X_{a}}x^{\prime}_{a}

Definition 15
.

The
interpretation
of an FMC-type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is the domain
\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
given by:

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket~\to\,\mathbb{N}^{\leq}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\qquad\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket=\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\times\dots\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\qquad\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket=\Pi_{a\in A}\,\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{a}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket

It is worth observing that for the simple types of the
\lambda
-calculus, as embedded in the FMC, these domains are the natural ones. Briefly (see
[
6
]
for details), a simple type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\dots\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}}\kern 1.0pto\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
embeds as the FMC-type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
with the domain
\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\times\dots\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\to\mathbb{N}^{\leq}
, which is the expected one modulo Currying.

Definition 16
.

The least element of a domain
\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
is written
0_{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}
. The
collapse
function
\lfloor-\rfloor_{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}:\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\to\mathbb{N}
takes a functional to a natural number by providing a least element as input and discarding all other output:
\lfloor f\rfloor_{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}=\pi_{1}(f(0_{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}))
.

We will interpret terms such that if
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
then
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, and if
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
at type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
then both
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\geq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
and
\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\rfloor>_{\mathbb{N}}\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\rfloor
, to give SN. We introduce the following notation.
To interpret terms in a context
\Gamma
, let a
valuation

v
on
\Gamma
be a function assigning to each variable
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
in
\Gamma
a value
v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
. The valuation
v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow t\}
assigns
t
to
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and otherwise behaves as
v
.
We write elements of product domains as vectors
(t_{1},\dots,t_{n})
, and will elide the isomorphisms for associativity and unitality so that concatenation of
s
and
t
may be written
(s,t)
. Concatenation lifts to indexed products pointwise:
(s,t)_{a}=(s_{a},t_{a})
. For
t\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
we have a singleton
a(t)\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\tau)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
where
a(t)_{a}=t
and
a(t)_{b}=()
for
b\neq a
.

Definition 17
.

For a term
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
, we inductively define the
interpretation

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
as follows, omitting
\Gamma
for compactness.

\begin{array}[]{@{}r@{}l@{}l@{\hspace{-10pt}}r@{}l}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}&(t)&{}=(0,t)\hfil\hskip-10.\\
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\,\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}&(s,r)&{}=(n\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0ptm,t)\hfil\hskip-10.&\text{where }(n,u)&{}=v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})(r)\\
&&\hfil\hskip-10.&(m,t)&{}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\,\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,u)\\
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\rho)\,\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}&(s,a(r))&{}=(1\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0ptm,t)\hfil\hskip-10.&\text{where }(m,t)&{}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)\\
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}&(s)&{}=(1\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0ptm\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\lfloor f\rfloor,t)\hfil\hskip-10.&\text{where }f&{}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\\
&&\hfil\hskip-10.&(m,t)&{}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\rho)\,\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(f))\end{array}

We write
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
for
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
with
v
the least valuation
v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})=0_{{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}}
, and may abbreviate
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
to
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
or
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
.

Remark 18
.

In this definition, the application case
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)=(1\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0ptm\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\lfloor f\rfloor)
adds the value
\lfloor f\rfloor
to account for reduction inside the argument
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. Further, both it and the abstraction case
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(r))=(1\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0ptm,t)
add
1
to count redexes, so that a reduction step reduces the overall measure by (at least)
2
. It would suffice to count only abstractions or only applications, but the choice to count both is so that we count steps of the stack machine. We observe the following: for the alternative interpretation that omits to count
\lfloor f\rfloor
, and instead has
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)=(1\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}\kern 1.0ptm)
, the collapsed interpretation
\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\rfloor
measures the exact length of machine runs for
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. This observation provides the proof with an operational intuition: terms are strongly normalizing because a) types guarantee termination of the machine
[
6
, Theorem 3.12]
, and b) reduction shortens the length of machine runs.

For the remainder of the proof, we will give an overview by stating the main lemmata. Each follows by a straightforward induction on typing derivations. First, for the interpretation
\llbracket-\rrbracket
to be well-defined, the construction for each term must be shown to preserve monotonicity. We will do so in the following lemma. For valuations
v
and
w
over
\Gamma
, let
v\leq w
denote that
v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}w({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
for all
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
in
\Gamma
.

Lemma 19
.

For all terms
\Gamma\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuations
v\leq w
over
\Gamma
, we have that:

1.

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket

2.

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}
.

For the next steps, we first need that the interpretation commutes with sequential composition
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{;}\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and substitution
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. Then, we show that reduction (non-strictly) decreases the interpretation, and strictly decreases the collapsed interpretation.

Lemma 20
.

For terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,r)=(i+j,u)\quad\text{where}\quad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(r)=(i,s)\quad\text{and}\quad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,s)=(j,u)~.

Lemma 21
.

For terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
,

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}~.

Lemma 22
.

If
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
then
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\geq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
for every valuation v on
\Gamma
.

Lemma 23
.

If
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
then
\pi_{1}(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s))>_{\mathbb{N}}\pi_{1}(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s))
for every
s\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
.

The last lemma then immediately gives the strong normalization result.

Theorem 24

(Strong Normalization)
.

Simply-typed FMC-terms are strongly normalizing with respect to beta-reduction.

Proof.

By Lemma
23
if
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
then
\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\rfloor>_{\mathbb{N}}\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\rfloor
, so that
\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket\rfloor
gives a bound for the length of any reduction path from
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
∎

Note that it is easy to extend this result to include eta-reduction: since eta-reduction does not increase the measure, and is clearly strongly normalizing by itself (the size of the term decreases), we can interleave each beta-reduction step with an arbitrary number of eta-reduction steps without affecting strong normalization.

6
Categorical semantics

We give the categorical view on the FMC in three layers:

•

terms with composition
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and unit
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
form a category;

•

terms modulo
\beta\eta
-equivalence form a
premonoidal
category
[
17
]
;

•

terms modulo an appropriate equational theory form a complete language for Cartesian closed categories;

we then show that
machine equivalence
, where terms are equivalent if they display the same input/output behaviour on the machine, validates the final equational theory.

The idea that a calculus with effects should semantically be a CCC may be surprising, so we will first motivate what the semantics does and does not capture. Firstly, and most importantly, the semantics we give here is one of the pure FMC, and emphatically
not
a semantics
of effects
: it ignores that, for instance,
input
only has a
pop
operation but no
push
, and that
state
locations would be restricted to a stack of depth one (at most). Imposing these constraints will cause the CCC semantics to break down, as we will demonstrate later in the section.

Secondly, the situation is analogous to the encoding of monadic effects in simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus, where for instance
state
encodes as the monad
S\to(-\times S)
. In that case, too, the semantics remains a CCC, despite the possibility of encoding effects.

Thirdly, the two generalizations of the FMC,
locations
and
sequencing
, remain close enough to the
\lambda
-calculus that simple types allow to collapse them back onto a CCC semantics.
Locations
give multiple copies of the
\lambda
-calculus, but because the types give the entire memory, the semantics may combine the different indexed stacks into one, projecting the multiple copies onto a single
\lambda
-calculus.
Sequencing
gives control over evaluation and allows us to encode various reduction strategies, but the point of the denotational perspective is precisely to collapse any computational behaviour, and only consider the input/output behaviour of a term.

The purpose of our CCC semantics is to demonstrate that the simply-typed FMC is an
operational
refinement of the lambda-calculus, but not a
denotational
one. The FMC allows to express
how
computation takes place: what reduction strategy is used, whether inputs are passed as function arguments or via mutable store, when the random generator is consulted,
etc.
The denotational perspective then collapses these distinctions, demonstrating that we remain firmly in the domain of higher-order functional computation, despite the ability to encode effects.

The plain category

For simplicity we will work in the sequential
\lambda
-calculus. The arguments generalize straightforwardly to the case of the FMC, and the details of this case are to be given in the first author’s Ph.D. thesis. The objects are then type vectors
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and morphisms in
\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma}\longrightarrow\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau}
will be
closed
terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
modulo the given equivalence.

A term
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\rho_{m}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\sigma_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\dots\sigma_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
may be represented by a string diagram as below, left. The wires represent the input and output stacks, with the first element at the top.
Strict composition of terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
into
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{;}\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, given below, right.

Analogous to the notation for type vectors, we introduce the following notation.
We use vector notation for variables,
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\dots{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, and reverse a vector by a left-pointing arrow: if
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is as before, then
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\dots{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. Concatenation of vectors is by juxtaposition. We lift the notation to sequences of abstractions and applications, but only for variables: if
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is as above, then
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle\dots\langle x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}]\dots[x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. Vector notation is extended to contexts as
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{1}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},\dots,}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau_{n}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~=~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and simultaneous substitutions as
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{S/\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{M_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}/x_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0},\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\dots,\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}/x_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, where
S=\varepsilon\cdot{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M_{1}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\cdots{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M_{n}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

The first, plain category is then given by Lemma
4
.

The premonoidal category

A
premonoidal
category
[
17
]
, like a monoidal category, describes string diagrams, but with a
sequential
element: a premonoidal product
\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt
has no
parallel
composition
f\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0ptg
, while
(f\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\mathsf{id});(\mathsf{id}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0ptg)
and
(\mathsf{id}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0ptg);(f\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\mathsf{id})
are distinct.

Formally, a premonoidal product is a binary operation on objects
X\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0ptY
that is a functor in each argument,
-\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0ptX
and
X\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt-
, but need not be a bifunctor
-\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt-
.
In the FMC, the action on objects is concatenation,
\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}~{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, with the first element at the top of the stack, and the unit given by
\varepsilon
. Both actions on morphisms are given below for
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

\begin{array}[]{rclc}M\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau}:~\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\rho}\longrightarrow\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}&{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\vbox{\hbox{$\hbox to83.72pt{\vbox to36.8pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 41.86115pt\lower-6.4pt\hbox The first is
expansion
(see Property 3.9 of the previous paper
[
6
]
). The second lifts the arguments for
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
from the stack as the variables
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, to restore them after evaluating
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. We illustrate these above.
A premonoidal product further has an
associator
and a
unitor
, and is called
strict
if these are identities, which they are here. The category is then formed by terms modulo
\beta\eta
-equivalence, where
\eta
-equivalence is generated by:

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~=_{\eta}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle x\rangle.\,[x].\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\quad\text{where }{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\notin\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})

Proposition 25
.

Terms modulo
\beta\eta
-equivalence form a strict premonoidal category.

We remark that terms modulo
\beta\eta
-equivalence do
not
form a
symmetric
pre-monoidal category, due to the failure of naturality of symmetry. Of course, one can add further equations to remedy this. In the sequel, we develop an extended equational theory which in fact makes the category of terms Cartesian closed.

Premonoidal structure forces a notion of sequentiality, which has previously been employed to capture that of effects, as in the closed Freyd categories of Power, Thielecke, and Levy
[
16
,
8
]
, which are the premonoidal equivalent of Cartesian closed categories. However, this imposed sequentiality is only necessary if interactions through effects (such as state) are hidden from the type system. Because the FMC makes these explicit, they can instead be accounted for in the semantics, which then reverts to a Cartesian closed category.

The Cartesian closed category

We now give an example illustrating why we would expect the FMC to form a Cartesian (closed) category, despite its ability to encode effects. For this example, but not for the rest of the section, we will then consider terms
with
locations. Note, first, how the two following terms are illustrated in string diagrams below.

Example 26
.

We introduce the following effectful operations as defined constructs (“sugar”) into the FMC: reading from a stream of random integers (
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\mathbb{Z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
),
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rand}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, a memory cell
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
with
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
operations, and writing to output,
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{write}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
We give the definitions and types of these operations and illustrate these as as (nominal) string diagrams below, using colours and dashed lines to indicate non-main locations:
red
for
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
,
blue
for
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
yellow
for
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{out}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. We use the black dot here to depict a transition from one location to another
1
1

1

This corresponds to the
renaming
a wire in the formalism of nominal string diagrams,
i.e.
, string diagrams where wires additionally have an associated
name
.
(as in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
,
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{write}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
), as well as to depict the terminal
!
(as in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
) and diagonal
\Delta
(as in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
), as is standard.

\begin{array}[]{cccc}\ \ {\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rand}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\quad{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\quad{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{write}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\\
\ \ {\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\langle x\rangle.\,[x]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\quad{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle x\rangle.\,c\langle\_\rangle.\,[x]c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\quad{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}c\langle x\rangle.\,[x]c.\,[x]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle x\rangle.\,[x]\mathsf{out}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\\
\ \ {\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\mathsf{rnd}(\mathbb{Z}){\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\mathbb{Z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\quad{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\mathbb{Z}c(\mathbb{Z}){\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}c(\mathbb{Z})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&\quad{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}c(\mathbb{Z}){\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}c(\mathbb{Z})\mathbb{Z}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}&{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\mathbb{Z}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\mathsf{out}(Z)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\\

Note that, modulo renaming of wires, these effectful operations are encoded by the diagonal and terminal operations of a Cartesian category.

Consider the following term (reprised from Examples
4.4
and
4.8
of the previous paper
[
6
]
) and its string diagrammatic representation. Note that we give the diagram modulo symmetry.
2
2

2

Note that, because operations acting on different locations permute, we have, for example, no need to lift (and restore) the arguments from the stacks at locations
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
prior to (and subsequent to) applying the second instances of
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rand}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
,
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
or
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
— or, equivalently, doing so has the same result as not doing so. Technically, the term corresponding to the diagram would lift the result of the first instance of
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
off the main
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
stack before applying the second instance of
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, and then restore it afterwards; however, since
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}+\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is symmetric in its inputs, we omit this for simplicity.

Due to the strong type system, we see all the dependencies between operations.
For example, the second call to
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rand}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
may safely be made before the first calls to
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. This would be illustrated by ‘sliding’ the
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rand}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
operation along the wire — something which is forbidden in general in a pre-monoidal category, but is permissible in a monoidal setting. We can also see that the second
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{set}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is dependent on the first
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{get}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. Indeed, their composition forms a beta-redex, corresponding to the expected interaction of
!
with
\Delta
in a Cartesian category.

Note, one can see in the above example that applying the naturality of the diagonal would result in a duplication on the location
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. This relies on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
being a location with no special status, and in particular, having an associated push action, similar to the main location
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\lambda\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. If we were to enforce that
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\mathsf{rnd}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
was a read-only stream, then this duplication would no longer be possible and the semantics can no longer be Cartesian. Similar issues arise for memory cells, which ought to have depth (at most) one. We leave consideration of the particular properties of encoded effects for future work.

The following data will make
\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\otimes$}}}}\kern 1.0pt
a Cartesian product
\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\times$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\times$}}}}\kern 1.0pt
, when considered modulo the following equational theory.
The
exponent

\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma}\kern 1.0pt{\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\rightarrow$}}}}\kern 1.0pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}~{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
will then give Cartesian closure.

Definition 27
.

We define the equational theory
=_{\textsf{eqn}}
of the FMC to be the least equivalence generated by the following laws, closed under all contexts.

Beta:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N].\,\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle=_{\beta}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\{N/x\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Interchange:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,N.\,[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle=_{\iota}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M.\,\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{y}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,N.\,[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{y}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Diagonal:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{y}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{y}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{y}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle=_{\Delta}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,M.\,[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Terminal:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M.\,\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{y}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle=_{!}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Eta (First-order):

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle=_{\eta}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle a\rangle.\,[a]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\alpha{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\alpha\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

Eta (Higher-order):

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle=_{\epsilon}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\rangle.\,[[\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{x}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}].\,P.\,\langle z\rangle.\,z]\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\alpha\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}(\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8})\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, and we do not allow abstractions to capture in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, or
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

Note that this theory includes beta- and eta-reduction. To see it includes eta-reduction at higher-type, consider the higher-order eta equation with
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
3
3

3

Following the definition of substitution, given a context
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\!-\!{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\}.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
with hole
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\!-\!{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, the substitution of a term
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
into the hole is given by
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, in particular with
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
not binding in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. This means the eta equations together give
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\langle x\rangle.\,[x].\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}={\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
.

Theorem 28
.

Terms modulo
=_{\textsf{eqn}}
form a strict Cartesian closed category.

The proof of the theorem above provides a canonical functor from the free Cartesian closed category generated over a set of base types
\Sigma
, denoted
\textup{{CCC}}(\Sigma)
, to the category of FMC terms generated over th same signature, denoted
\Lambda S/{{\textsf{eqn}}}
. We construct a left-inverse CCC-functor interpreting FMC-terms into
\lambda
-terms (with products and patterns), thus proving completeness. In general, all constructions also work with also with constants drawn from a monoidal signature, as well as simply with a signature given by a set of base types.

The interpretation
\llbracket-\rrbracket:\Lambda S/{{\textsf{eqn}}}\to\textup{{CCC}}(\Sigma)
preserves types.
The top-level arrow
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
of FMC-types becomes sequent entailment
\vdash
: the type of the input stack becomes the type of the
\lambda
-context and the type of the output stack becomes the type of the
\lambda
-term.

A
valuation
v
is a function assigning to each FMC-variable
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
a
\lambda
-term
v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
.
Given a valuation
v
, let
v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow t\}
denote the valuation which assigns
t
to
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and otherwise behaves as
v
. We write contexts and products as vectors and elide the isomorphisms for associativity and unitality so that concatenation of
s
and
t
may be written as
s\cdot t
.

.
