Theorem 30
.

Terms modulo
=_{\textsf{eqn}}
form a complete language for Cartesian closed categories.

Machine Equivalence

A natural contextual equivalence on terms is given by
machine equivalence
, defined inductively on types below. It resembles the logical relation for program equivalence of Pitts and Stark
[
12
]
. We write
(S,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}){\Downarrow}
for
T
if
(S,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\kern 1.0pt{\Downarrow}\kern 1.0pt(T,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
and take here the only constants to be of base type.

Definition 31
.

Closed terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
are
machine equivalent at type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
if for equivalent inputs the machine gives equivalent outputs,

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M{\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sim\,}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\scriptscriptstyle\Delta}}{{=}}~\forall\,S{\sim}S^{\prime}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}.~(S,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}){\Downarrow}\sim(S^{\prime},{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}){\Downarrow}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

where two terms of base type are equivalent if they are equal, and two stacks are equivalent if their terms are pairwise equivalent. Equivalence extends to open terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
as follows:
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{w}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\omega\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M{\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sim\,}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
if and only if

\forall\,W{\sim}W^{\prime}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\omega\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}.~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{W/\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{w}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\sim{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{W^{\prime}/\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{w}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~.

Machine equivalence validates the equational theory (and in particular the beta and eta equations). Thus we have the following result.

Theorem 32
.

Terms modulo machine equivalence form a Cartesian closed category.

In fact, the category given by terms modulo machine equivalence is just the extensional collapse of the category of terms modulo the equational theory. Note that machine equivalence is strictly coarser than the equational theory: a situation analogous to that of the simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus with products, considered modulo an appropriate contextual equivalence.

7
Further work

The current type system for the FMC is
too strong
for practical programming: it captures such intensional (and unobservable) aspects of computation as the number of elements read from a random stream. We aim to investigate more abstract type systems, including dealing with the particular properties of effectful locations. The results in this paper concerning the type system, which is essentially a presentation of intuitionistic logic, the operational intuition and the close denotational relationship with the
\lambda
-calculus make a strong basis for future refinements which account properly for effects. There are several ways in which we already know how to weaken the type system: introducing a recursor, stream types
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}^{*}
, which type a stream of terms of type
{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, and ignoring types on non-main locations. A close link with string diagrams is evident from the results presented, including with the recently introduced higher-order string diagrams for CCCs
[
1
]
. This is another avenue for investigation.

Appendix A
Details for Section
5
: Strong Normalization

We re-state and prove the lemmata for the strong normalization proof in Section
5
.

Theorem 33

(Lemma
19
restatement)
.

For all terms
\Gamma\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuations
v\leq w
over
\Gamma
, we have that:

1.

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket

2.

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}
.

Proof.

We prove both statements simultaneously by induction on the type derivation of
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
Recall that the first item is equivalent to claiming
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
is monotonic. When we write ‘increasing’ here, we mean non-strictly.

•

For the base case

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

1.

Observe that
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
(t) = (0, t) and so is monotonic.

2.

Observe that
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}

for every
v
and
w
over
\Gamma
.

•

For the abstraction case, where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\rho)\,\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

1.

We must show the function

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(r))=(1+n,t)\textup{ where }(n,t)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)\

is monotonic. Indeed, we have that
(s,a(r))\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\,a(\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(s^{\prime},a(r^{\prime}))
implies, by inductive hypothesis (ii) on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, that

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r^{\prime}\}}\ ,

and then by inductive hypothesis
(i)
on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, this implies

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r^{\prime}\}}(s^{\prime})\ .

Thus, increasing the input of the function increases its output.

2.

We wish to show, for arbitrary
(s,a(r))\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\,a(\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, that

v\leq w\quad\textup{implies}\quad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(r))\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,a(r))\ .

Unfolding definitions, we see we must show that
v\leq w
implies

\displaystyle(1+n,t)

\displaystyle\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(1+n^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)\ ,

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n,^{\prime}t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)\

By assumption, we have that, for all
r\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
,
v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}\leq w\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}
. Applying inductive hypothesis
(ii)
on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we thus have that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}\ .

and consequently
(n,t)\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
.
Indeed, the required result
immediately follows.

•

For the application case, with
\Gamma\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and

\displaystyle\Gamma\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

1.

We have to show that

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)

\displaystyle=(1+n+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor,t)

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}))\ .

is monotonic. Applying inductive hypothesis
(i)
on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we achieve that
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
is monotonic. Thus, increasing the input
s
increases
(n,t)
, which therefore increases the output of the entire function.

2.

We have to show, for arbitrary
s\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, that

v\leq w\qquad\textup{implies}\qquad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s)

Unfolding definitions, we see we must show that
v\leq w
implies

\displaystyle(1+n+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor,t)

\displaystyle\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(1+n^{\prime}+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}\rfloor,t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,a(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}))\ .

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,a(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}))\ .

Applying inductive hypothesis
(ii)
on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we achieve

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a(\rho)\,\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}\quad\textup{and}\quad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}.

The conjunction of both statements implies that

(n,t)\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
.
We aditionaly observe that
\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor\leq_{\mathbb{N}}\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}\rfloor
, and the result follows.

•

For the variable case,

\displaystyle\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

1.

We have to show that

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,r)

\displaystyle=(n+m,t)

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m,t){}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,u)

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n,u){}

\displaystyle=v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})(r)\ .

is monotonic.
Observe that
v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
and so is monotonic. Thus, increasing the input
(s,r)
increases
(n,u)
. We have from inductive hypothesis (i) on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
that
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
is monotonic. Altogether, this results in an increase in
(m,t)
, which therefore increases the output of the entire function.

2.

We wish to show, for arbitrary
(s,r)\in{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, that

v\leq w\qquad\textup{implies}\qquad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,r)\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,r)\ .

Unfolding definitions, we see we must show that
v\leq w
implies

\displaystyle(n+m,t)

\displaystyle\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n^{\prime}+m^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m,t){}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,u)

\displaystyle\textup{and }\ \ (n,u){}

\displaystyle=v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})(r)

\displaystyle\textup{and }(m,^{\prime}t^{\prime}){}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n,^{\prime}u^{\prime}){}

\displaystyle=w({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})(r)\ .

By assumption, we have
v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}w({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
, which implies that

(n,u)\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n^{\prime},u^{\prime})
. Applying inductive hypothesis (ii) on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we have that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\leq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.60275pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.36165pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}.

Altogether, this implies
(m,t)\leq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(m^{\prime},t^{\prime})
.
Thus, we achieve the required result.
∎

We add the following
Weakening Lemma
.

Lemma 34

(Weakening)
.

For all terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, valuations
v
on
\Gamma
, and
s\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, we have that

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow s\}}

where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\notin\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
.

Proof.

Induction on the type derivation of
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.
∎

Theorem 35

(Lemma
20
restatement)
.

For terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}\!{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,r)=(i+j,u)\quad\text{where}\quad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(r)=(i,s)\quad\text{and}\quad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,s)=(j,u)~.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the type derivation of
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

Case

v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})(n)=(i,p)\qquad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(r,p)=(j,s)\qquad\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,s)=(k,u)

so that

. The inductive hypothesis gives
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,r,p)=(j+k,u)
, so that

as required.

Case

Case

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
is not free in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
(otherwise,
\alpha
-rename it in
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

34
also
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow p\}}(t,s)=(j,u)
. The inductive hypothesis gives
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow p\}}(t,r)=(i+j,u)
, so that
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,(N\,{;}\,M)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t,r,p)=(1+i+j,u)
, as required.
∎

Theorem 36

(Lemma
21
restatement)
.

For terms
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\sigma~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
,

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}~.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the type derivation of
\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
.

•

For the base case,

\displaystyle\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

observe that
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Delta}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
is independent of
v
and of
\Delta
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(t)=(0,t)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\star\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}(t)\ ,

as required.

•

For the abstraction case,

\displaystyle\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle y\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\rho)\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

we need to show that

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}a\langle y\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(r))=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle y\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}(s,a(r))\ ,

where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\neq{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
. For the left-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}a\langle y\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},a(r))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle y\rangle.\,\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},a(r))

\displaystyle=(1+n,{t})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,{t})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}({s})\ ,

and, for the right-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle y\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s},a(r))

\displaystyle=(1+n^{\prime},{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n^{\prime},{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{u\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s})\ .

Let
u=v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}
.
Applying the inductive hypothesis on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
gives the first equality below.

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{u\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{u}\}}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{u\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}

By Lemma
34
(the Weakening Lemma),
and since
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
, we have
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{u}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
. This gives the second equality.
Thus, we have that
(n,t)=(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
and the required result follows.

•

For the application case, where
\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}c\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and

\displaystyle\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[P]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

we need to show that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}[P]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[P]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}(s)\ .

For the left-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}[P]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[\{N/x\}P]a.\,\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s})

\displaystyle=(1+n+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\{N/x\}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor,t)

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},a(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}))\ ,

and, for the right-hand side, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[P]a.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s})

\displaystyle=(1+n^{\prime}+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}\rfloor,t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s},a(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}))\ .

Applying the inductive hypothesis on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
achieves

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}\ .

Thus, we have that
(n,t)=(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
and indeed
1+n^{\prime}+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor=1+n^{\prime}+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}\rfloor
as required.

For the variable case, where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\neq{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\omega\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

we need to show that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}y.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,r)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}(s,r)\ .

For the left-hand side,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}y.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y.\,\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},{r})

\displaystyle=(n+m,{t})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m,{t})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},{u})

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n,{u})

\displaystyle=v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})({r})\ ,

and for the right-hand side,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s},{r})

\displaystyle=(n^{\prime}+m^{\prime},{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m^{\prime},{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s},{u^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n^{\prime},{u^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})({r})\ .

Observe that
v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})=v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}y\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
, which implies
(n,u)=(n^{\prime},u^{\prime})
.
Application of the inductive hypothesis on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
achieves

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}\ .

Thus, we have
(m,t)=(m^{\prime},t^{\prime})
and the result follows.

For the variable case,

\displaystyle\Gamma,{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\vdash{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\equiv{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

we need to show that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}x.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,r)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}(s,r)\ .

For the left-hand side, by application of the Sequencing Lemma
20
, we have

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},{r})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\,{;}\,\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},{r})

\displaystyle=(n+m,{t})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m,{t})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},{u})

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n,{u})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({r})\ ,

and, for the right-hand side,

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s},{r})

\displaystyle=(n^{\prime}+m^{\prime},{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m^{\prime},{t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}({s},{u^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n^{\prime},{u^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})({r})\ .

Observe that
v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
, which implies
(n,u)=(n^{\prime},u^{\prime})
.
Application of the inductive hypothesis on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
achieves

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}\ .

Thus, we have
(m,t)=(m^{\prime},t^{\prime})
and the result follows.
∎

We simplify the remaining proof by separating the
\beta
-rule

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,H.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}H.\,\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

into a
permutation
part

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,H.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\sim_{p}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}H.\,[N]a.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

and a
strict reduction
part

{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~.

The
permutation equivalence

\sim
on terms is given by:

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[P]a.\,[N]b.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]b.\,[P]a.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,[N]b.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]b.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle\text{if }x\notin\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,b\langle y\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

\displaystyle\sim_{p}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}b\langle y\rangle.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

where in each case
a\neq b
. We first show that the interpretation of terms is preserved under permutations (the
Permutation Lemma
).

Lemma 37

(Permutation)
.

For
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sim_{p}\,\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
and any valuation
v
on
\Gamma
,
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
.

Proof.

The most interesting case is

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,[N]b.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\sim_{p}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]b.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\rho)\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\ ,

where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
.
To see this, observe the following are equivalent:

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,[N]b.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},a(r))

\displaystyle=(n+2+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}\rfloor,{t}),

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,{t})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}({s},b(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}),

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]b.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}({s},a(r))

\displaystyle=(n+2+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor,{t}),

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,{t})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}P\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}({s},b(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}),

using Lemma
34
, the Weakening Lemma (since
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\not\in\mathsf{fv}({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})
). The other cases follow immediately from unfolding definitions.
∎

Theorem 38

(Lemma
22
restatement)
.

If
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\,\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
then
\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\geq_{\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\tau\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}
for every valuation v on
\Gamma
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of one-step reductions. Using the Permutation Lemma
37
, we need only consider the
strict

\beta
-rule in the base case.

•

The base case,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

where
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\rho\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
requires showing, for arbitrary
s\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, that

\displaystyle\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)~\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}~\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)\ .

Unfolding definitions, we must show

\displaystyle(2+n+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor,t)

\displaystyle\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}~\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{x\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}(s)\ .

Applying the Substitution Lemma
21
,

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{x\leftarrow\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\}}=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v},

we see
(n,t)=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}\{N/x\}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s)
.
Observing that
(2+n+\lfloor\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}\rfloor,t)\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n,t)
, the result follows.

•

The abstraction case,

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\rho)\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

requires showing, for arbitrary
(s,a(r))\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}a\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}(\rho)\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(r))~\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}~\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}a\langle x\rangle.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,a(r))\ .

Unfolding definitions, we must show

\displaystyle(1+n,t)

\displaystyle\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(1+n^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(n,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}}(s)\ .

Applying the inductive hypothesis on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we achieve

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s)\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s)\ ,

for any valuation
w
. In particular, we can set
w=\{{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\leftarrow r\}

and thus we have that
(n,t)\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n^{\prime},t^{\prime})
. The result follows.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}~{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}:{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}},

with
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\colon\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\leftharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}{\kern 1.0pt\mathchoice{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.86108pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\raise 0.51663pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}{\mathbin{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\Rightarrow$}}}\kern 1.0pt}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}

requires showing, for arbitrary
(s,r)\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\sigma\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\rho\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
, that

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,r)~\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}~\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x.\,M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,r)\ .

Unfolding definitions, we must show

\displaystyle(n+m,t)

\displaystyle\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n+m^{\prime},t^{\prime})

\displaystyle\textup{where }(m,t)

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,u)

\displaystyle\textup{and }(m,^{\prime}t^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s,u)

\displaystyle\textup{and }(n,u)

\displaystyle=v({\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}x\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}})(r)

Applying the inductive hypothesis on
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{
}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L 10.2 0}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{7.5pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 3.07 0 L 0.35 2.4 L 0 0 L 0.35 -2.4 Z}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}
, we achieve, for any
u\in\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\upsilon\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket
,

\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,u)\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M^{\prime}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{w}(s,u)\ ,

and thus that
(m,t)\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(m^{\prime},t^{\prime})
and consequently
(n+m,t)\geq_{\mathbb{N}\times\llbracket{\vphantom{)}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\kern 0.4pt\accentset{\rightharpoonup}{\tau\kern 0.4pt}{}_{A}\color[rgb]{0,0,0.8}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket}(n+m^{\prime},t^{\prime})
, as required.
∎

Theorem 39

(Lemma
23
restatement)
.

If
{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}~{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}~\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}

then
\pi_{1}(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s))>_{\mathbb{N}}\pi_{1}(\llbracket{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}N\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}\rrbracket_{v}(s))
for every

and valuation
v
on
\Gamma
.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on the derivation of one-step reductions. Using the Permutation Lemma
37
, we need only consider the
strict

\beta
-rule in the base case.

\displaystyle{\vphantom{(}\color[rgb]{0.8,0,0}[N]a.\,a\langle x\rangle.\,M\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}}~\mathrel{\hbox to10.5pt{\vbox to4.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.25pt\lower-2.25pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}
